"""Bind-address resolution + DNS-rebinding Host-header guard (PACKAGING_PLAN.md P0c)."""

import pytest

pytest.importorskip("httpx")  # TestClient needs httpx
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

import backend.main as m
from backend.config import AppConfig


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def _clear_env(monkeypatch):
    for var in ("RALLY_BIND_HOST", "RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS"):
        monkeypatch.delenv(var, raising=False)


# ---- bind host resolution ----

def test_bind_defaults_to_localhost():
    assert m._resolve_bind_host(AppConfig(), None) == "127.0.0.1"


def test_bind_cli_host_wins(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setenv("RALLY_BIND_HOST", "10.0.0.5")
    cfg = AppConfig()
    cfg.security.edge_auth_enabled = True
    assert m._resolve_bind_host(cfg, "192.168.1.9") == "192.168.1.9"  # --host beats env + profile


def test_bind_env_beats_profile(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setenv("RALLY_BIND_HOST", "0.0.0.0")
    assert m._resolve_bind_host(AppConfig(), None) == "0.0.0.0"


def test_bind_edge_auth_profile_widens_to_all_interfaces():
    cfg = AppConfig()
    cfg.security.edge_auth_enabled = True
    assert m._resolve_bind_host(cfg, None) == "0.0.0.0"


def test_bind_no_config_is_localhost():
    assert m._resolve_bind_host(None, None) == "127.0.0.1"


# ---- allowed-hosts resolution ----

def test_allowed_hosts_default_is_loopback_plus_testserver():
    hosts = m._allowed_hosts_from_env({})
    assert "127.0.0.1" in hosts and "localhost" in hosts and "testserver" in hosts
    assert "*" not in hosts


def test_allowed_hosts_env_override():
    assert m._allowed_hosts_from_env({"RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS": "app.example.com, 1.2.3.4"}) == [
        "app.example.com", "1.2.3.4"]


def test_allowed_hosts_wildcard_for_edge():
    assert m._allowed_hosts_from_env({"RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS": "*"}) == ["*"]


# ---- the middleware actually rejects a foreign Host (DNS-rebinding) ----

def test_foreign_host_header_is_rejected():
    client = TestClient(m.app)
    r = client.get("/api/health", headers={"host": "evil.attacker.com"})
    assert r.status_code == 400  # TrustedHostMiddleware: Invalid host header


def test_loopback_host_header_is_accepted():
    client = TestClient(m.app)
    # default TestClient host is "testserver" (allowed); an explicit loopback host (with port) too.
    assert client.get("/api/health").status_code == 200
    assert client.get("/api/health", headers={"host": "127.0.0.1:8000"}).status_code == 200

Let me compile the findings.

## Review findings

**[minor] backend/main.py:90 (`_allowed_hosts_from_env`) + Starlette trustedhost.py:40 — IPv6 loopback Host headers are silently rejected.** The default allowlist includes `"::1"` and `"[::1]"`, but Starlette's `TrustedHostMiddleware` matches with `host = headers.get("host","").split(":")[0]`. A real IPv6 Host header is `"[::1]:8000"` → `.split(":")[0]` yields `"["` (and bare `"::1"` → `""`), neither of which matches any allowlist entry. Empirically verified: `[::1]:8000`, `[::1]`, and `::1` all return **400**. So if a user binds/visits the appliance over IPv6 loopback, every request is wrongly rejected. The `"::1"`/`"[::1]"` entries in the default are dead — they can never match given Starlette's port-stripping. Fix: document that IPv6 loopback is unsupported by this guard, or (cleaner) front it with a host-normalizer. The prompt flagged this as minor and it is — IPv4 loopback (the actual default bind `127.0.0.1`) works fine.

**[minor] backend/config/startup.py:91-139 vs backend/main.py:96-100 — the edge-auth exposure footgun is surfaced only as a soft print, not enforced like its sibling checks.** When `edge_auth_enabled=True`, `_resolve_bind_host` returns `0.0.0.0` (main.py:114-115) but the TrustedHost allowlist is frozen at import from `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` only (main.py:98) — it cannot see config. If `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is unset, the loopback-only default 400s **100%** of forwarded (public-Host) requests from the edge gate. The `_exposure_checks` startup gate already FATALs on a missing `allowed_video_root` and missing `cf_team_domain`/`cf_access_aud`, but does **not** check the host allowlist — so a half-configured exposure (host-allowlist unset) starts "healthy" then 400s every real request. The only mitigation is the bind-time `print()` warning (main.py:823-827), which does correctly fire for `0.0.0.0` (verified: `0.0.0.0` is not in the suppress-tuple). Fix: add an `include_exposure` startup check that warns/errors when `edge_auth_enabled` and `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is unset/loopback-only, mirroring the existing `EXPOSED_*` checks — that's the cleaner option than the env-only docstring note. Not a blocker because the default path is unaffected and the print does surface it.

**[nit] backend/main.py:822 — default server-start banner string changed from `localhost` to the resolved bind host.** Was `http://localhost:{port}`; now `http://{bind_host}:{port}` which prints `http://127.0.0.1:8000` on the default path. The *bind* is byte-identical (`uvicorn.run` got `127.0.0.1` before too), but the user-visible banner text changed `localhost`→`127.0.0.1`. Purely cosmetic; flagging only because the contract says "byte-identical." No functional impact.

### Items checked and CLEARED (no issue)
- **Concern 1 — legitimate access:** `testserver` is allowlisted (main.py:90) → full TestClient suite passes (247 API tests + 10 new = green). Loopback Host **with port** `127.0.0.1:8000` → **200** (Starlette strips the port at trustedhost.py:40). Health, static `/`, and a good-host CORS preflight all return 200. No legitimate IPv4 path is wrongly 400'd.
- **Concern 3 — middleware order & CORS:** Verified via `build_middleware_stack` that the wrap order is ServerError → **TrustedHost → CORS** → router, i.e. TrustedHost is **outermost** and runs *before* CORS (FastAPI prepends; the stack is built inner-first via `reversed`). Empirically, a preflight `OPTIONS` from a disallowed Host returns 400 with **no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` header leaked**. `www_redirect=False` is correct — it prevents a 307 to a `www.` host (an open-redirect-ish surprise) and just 400s instead.
- **Concern 2 (precedence) — `_resolve_bind_host` order cli > env > profile > 127.0.0.1:** correct and safe; matches the four tests (`test_bind_cli_host_wins`, `test_bind_env_beats_profile`, `test_bind_edge_auth_profile_widens_to_all_interfaces`, `test_bind_defaults_to_localhost`).
- **Concern 4 — null-safety:** `getattr(config, "security", None)` then `getattr(sec, "edge_auth_enabled", False)` is null-safe for `config=None` AND `security=None` → returns `127.0.0.1` (proven by `test_bind_no_config_is_localhost`). The `--host` default `None` flows through `if cli_host and cli_host.strip()` correctly.
- **Hard contract (byte-identity of default path):** Holds. Default bind = `127.0.0.1` (unchanged), default allowlist preserves `testserver`, import does zero filesystem I/O (env-only), and the full existing TestClient suite is green. The only default-path delta is the cosmetic banner string above.

**Verdict:** Not a blocker — the hard contract holds and the default path is behaviorally byte-identical; ship-able, but consider promoting the edge-auth host-allowlist footgun (finding 2) to a startup check for parity with the existing `EXPOSED_*` gates, and document the IPv6-loopback limitation (finding 1).

### 251. user (2026-06-21T15:30:53.464Z)

90	def _exposure_checks(config: Any) -> list[StartupCheck]:
91	    """Exposure-safety posture for an *exposed* HTTP server. Gated on ``security.edge_auth_enabled``
92	    (default False → ``[]`` = strict no-op, today's behaviour). When edge auth is ON — the "I am
93	    exposing this engine behind the CF-Access gate" signal — the gate alone isn't enough: verify the
94	    rest of the posture at boot so a half-configured exposure fails fast instead of leaking / 500-ing
95	    per request. Config-only (no IO beyond the ``find_spec`` auth-extra probe)."""
96	    sec = getattr(config, "security", None)
97	    if not sec or not getattr(sec, "edge_auth_enabled", False):
98	        return []  # DEFAULT-OFF: edge auth off → no posture enforcement (byte-identical to today).
99	
100	    out: list[StartupCheck] = []
101	
102	    # (1) The path-jail is load-bearing once authenticated tenants can hit /api/process: WITHOUT it,
103	    # a tenant can point the engine at an ARBITRARY local file (allowed_video_root unset = "any local
104	    # file", the single-user default). Fatal for an exposed engine.
105	    root = getattr(sec, "allowed_video_root", None)
106	    if not root or not str(root).strip():
107	        out.append(StartupCheck(
108	            LEVEL_ERROR, "EXPOSED_NO_VIDEO_ROOT",
109	            "security.edge_auth_enabled is ON (engine exposed) but security.allowed_video_root is "
110	            "unset — an authenticated tenant could make /api/process read an arbitrary local file. "
111	            "Set allowed_video_root to a jail directory before exposing the engine.",
112	        ))
113	
114	    # (2) Edge auth cannot verify a token without the team domain + AUD. Today that only fails on the
115	    # FIRST request (auth.resolve_tenant); lift it to BOOT so a misconfigured gate never starts
116	    # "healthy" then 401s every request.
117	    team = (getattr(sec, "cf_team_domain", "") or "").strip()
118	    aud = (getattr(sec, "cf_access_aud", "") or "").strip()
119	    if not team or not aud:
120	        missing = " + ".join(n for n, v in (("cf_team_domain", team), ("cf_access_aud", aud)) if not v)
121	        out.append(StartupCheck(
122	            LEVEL_ERROR, "EXPOSED_EDGE_AUTH_INCOMPLETE",
123	            f"security.edge_auth_enabled is ON but {missing} is not configured — the Cf-Access JWT "
124	            "cannot be verified (every request would fail). Set security.cf_team_domain "
125	            "(e.g. https://<team>.cloudflareaccess.com) and security.cf_access_aud (the CF Access "
126	            "application AUD tag).",
127	        ))
128	
129	    # (3) The auth extra (PyJWT[crypto]) is lazy-imported by auth.verify_cf_access_token. If it's
130	    # missing, every request 500s at verify time — a boot WARN beats a per-request 500. Warning, not
131	    # fatal: the auth module's own ImportError is the final authority.
132	    if not _auth_extra_available():
133	        out.append(StartupCheck(
134	            LEVEL_WARN, "EXPOSED_AUTH_EXTRA_MISSING",
135	            "security.edge_auth_enabled is ON but PyJWT[crypto] is not importable — Cf-Access "
136	            "verification will fail at request time. Install the 'auth' extra (pip install -e .[auth]).",
137	        ))
138	
139	    return out
140

164	    ``include_exposure`` adds the exposure-safety posture (``_exposure_checks``) — the HTTP **server**
165	    passes ``True``; the worker leaves it ``False`` (it is never "exposed"). Both dimensions are
166	    DEFAULT-OFF: no profile → no profile checks; ``edge_auth_enabled=False`` → no exposure checks.

193	                f"'{storage_backend}'. Reads will go to a remote backend.",
194	            ))
195	        # GPU heads-up (warning only — the detector healthcheck is the authority; the probe is
196	        # best-effort and may be False even when a subprocess detector env has CUDA).
197	        if cuda_available is False:
198	            if device == "cuda":
199	                checks.append(StartupCheck(
200	                    LEVEL_WARN, "CUDA_NOT_VISIBLE",
201	                    "device='cuda' but no CUDA GPU is visible to this process. If the box truly "
202	                    "has no GPU the detector healthcheck will hard-fail — set indexing.wasb.device="
203	                    "'auto' for the CPU fallback (M4).",
204	                ))

28	      - name: Install ruff

30	      - name: Ruff check

32	
33	  # Type gate (mypy.ini at repo root): lenient green baseline with an explicit
34	  # per-module quarantine list — ratchet by removing entries as modules clean
35	  # up. Typed core deps are installed so mypy sees their real signatures
36	  # (without them ignore_missing_imports would Any-stub half the checks).
37	  typecheck:
38	    name: Mypy (lenient baseline)

40	    steps:
41	      - uses: actions/checkout@v6
42	      - uses: actions/setup-python@v6
43	        with:

46	        run: python -m pip install mypy pydantic fastapi uvicorn python-dotenv google-genai numpy
47	      - name: Mypy
48	        run: mypy backend training
49	
50	  # Fast guard against the 7fbbeb0 class of breakage: a SyntaxError or
51	  # module-level import error shipping while the suite stays green.

54	  import-smoke:

59	      - uses: actions/setup-python@v6

62	      - name: Install minimal deps

64	      - name: Run import smoke tests
65	        run: python -m pytest tests/test_import_smoke.py -v
66	
67	  full-suite:
68	    name: Full test suite (offline, mocked)

70	    steps:

73	        with:

76	      - name: System libs for opencv-python

78	          sudo apt-get update

80	      - name: Install CPU-only torch (no CUDA wheels on a CPU runner)
81	        # torch/torchvision are NOT in pyproject deps — their CPU/CUDA wheels

84	        run: python -m pip install torch torchvision --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu
85	      - name: Install package (editable) + dev tooling

88	        run: python -m pip install -e .[dev]

95	      # without an owner decision.
96	      - name: Run full suite (with coverage floor)

98	
99	  # Cross-OS determinism guard for the committed golden regression fixtures

101	  # (only numpy, no cv2/torch), so this is a FAST, focused job — it does NOT run
102	  # the full suite x3 OSes. It replays the Windows/Linux/macOS runner against the
103	  # committed (parse-compared) snapshots, catching any cross-OS float drift beyond
104	  # the 1e-6 tolerance before it can flake a contributor's PR on another machine.
105	  golden-crossos:
106	    name: Golden fixtures cross-OS (${{ matrix.os }})

118	        run: python -m pip install pytest fastapi uvicorn pydantic python-dotenv google-genai numpy
119	      - name: Golden-fixtures characterization (cross-OS determinism guard)

### 333. assistant (2026-06-21T15:57:19.710Z)

"""Single-instance file lock (PACKAGING_PLAN.md P0e).

The serving model runs an IN-PROCESS job worker, and ``fail_stale_jobs()`` sweeps any job left
``queued``/``running`` by a "previous" process to ``failed`` on startup. If a SECOND process opens the
same SQLite DB (a second ``indexer --server``, or a CLI run while a server is up), its sweep would
mark the FIRST process's in-flight jobs failed — corrupting live state. This module guards the DB
with an exclusive OS lock so the second instance refuses to start instead.

OS-managed advisory lock (``fcntl.flock`` on POSIX, ``msvcrt.locking`` on Windows): held for the
process lifetime via an open file handle and **released automatically by the OS on exit OR crash**,
so there is no stale-lock problem (unlike a bare PID file). The lock is keyed to the lock-file path,
which callers co-locate with the DB — so two instances on DIFFERENT ``--output-dir`` (different DBs)
coexist fine.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
from typing import IO, Optional

_IS_WINDOWS = os.name == "nt"


def acquire_lock(lock_path: str) -> Optional[IO]:
    """Try to take an exclusive, NON-blocking lock on ``lock_path``.

    Returns the open file handle on success (KEEP IT ALIVE for the process lifetime — letting it be
    garbage-collected/closed releases the lock), or ``None`` if another live process holds it.
    """
    parent = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(lock_path))
    if parent:
        os.makedirs(parent, exist_ok=True)
    fh = open(lock_path, "a+")
    try:
        if _IS_WINDOWS:
            import msvcrt
            fh.seek(0)
            msvcrt.locking(fh.fileno(), msvcrt.LK_NBLCK, 1)  # lock 1 byte at offset 0, non-blocking
        else:
            import fcntl
            fcntl.flock(fh.fileno(), fcntl.LOCK_EX | fcntl.LOCK_NB)
    except OSError:
        fh.close()
        return None
    # Record our PID for human diagnostics (best-effort; the lock — not this content — is authoritative).
    try:
        fh.seek(0)
        fh.truncate()
        fh.write(str(os.getpid()))
        fh.flush()
    except OSError:
        pass
    return fh


def release_lock(fh: Optional[IO]) -> None:
    """Release a lock taken by :func:`acquire_lock` (the OS also releases it on process exit)."""
    if fh is None:
        return
    try:
        if _IS_WINDOWS:
            import msvcrt
            fh.seek(0)
            msvcrt.locking(fh.fileno(), msvcrt.LK_UNLCK, 1)
        else:
            import fcntl
            fcntl.flock(fh.fileno(), fcntl.LOCK_UN)
    except OSError:
        pass
    finally:
        try:
            fh.close()
        except OSError:
            pass

### 344. assistant (2026-06-21T15:58:12.384Z)

"""Single-instance OS lock (PACKAGING_PLAN.md P0e)."""

import os

from backend.utils.single_instance import acquire_lock, release_lock


def test_acquire_then_second_acquire_is_blocked(tmp_path):
    lock = str(tmp_path / "db.sqlite.lock")
    fh = acquire_lock(lock)
    assert fh is not None, "first acquire should succeed"
    try:
        # A second acquire on the SAME lock path (even from this process, via a new handle) must fail
        # while the first is held — that's the second-instance-refused guarantee.
        assert acquire_lock(lock) is None
    finally:
        release_lock(fh)


def test_release_allows_reacquire(tmp_path):
    lock = str(tmp_path / "db.sqlite.lock")
    fh = acquire_lock(lock)
    assert fh is not None
    release_lock(fh)
    fh2 = acquire_lock(lock)  # released → free again
    assert fh2 is not None
    release_lock(fh2)


def test_different_paths_dont_conflict(tmp_path):
    a = acquire_lock(str(tmp_path / "a.lock"))
    b = acquire_lock(str(tmp_path / "b.lock"))  # different DB/output-dir → independent
    assert a is not None and b is not None
    release_lock(a)
    release_lock(b)


def test_acquire_creates_parent_dir(tmp_path):
    nested = tmp_path / "deep" / "nested" / "db.lock"
    fh = acquire_lock(str(nested))
    try:
        assert fh is not None
        assert os.path.exists(str(nested))
    finally:
        release_lock(fh)


def test_lockfile_records_pid(tmp_path):
    lock = str(tmp_path / "db.lock")
    fh = acquire_lock(lock)
    try:
        with open(lock, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            assert f.read().strip() == str(os.getpid())
    finally:
        release_lock(fh)

### 362. user (2026-06-21T16:05:17.014Z)

One more consideration on the lock lifecycle: on the CLI single-shot path, main() returns normally (e.g. line 769, 837) without ever calling `release_lock`. The lock relies on the OS releasing it at process exit. That's fine and documented — but worth confirming there's no in-process workflow where main() returns and the process keeps running (e.g. main() called twice in one process, or a test importing main). The subprocess tests spawn fresh processes, so fine. In-process, `_instance_lock` would stay held until process exit; a second `main()` call in the same process would `acquire_lock` the same path on a *new* handle — which on POSIX (flock) is associated per-open-file-description so a new `open()` + flock would **block/fail** against the same process's existing lock (flock conflicts across different fds even in the same process), and on Windows msvcrt also conflicts across handles. This only matters for a hypothetical double-`main()`-in-one-process, not a real workflow. Noting as informational, not a finding.

I have enough to write the review.

---

## Findings

**[minor] backend/utils/single_instance.py:35 — `open(lock_path, "a+")` is OUTSIDE the try/except.** If the lock directory/path is not writable (read-only app-home, permission error, path is a directory), `open()` raises `OSError`/`PermissionError` which propagates out of `acquire_lock` and crashes `main()` with an unhandled traceback — instead of the clean `[FATAL] … Refusing to start` message the design intends for the "can't run here" case. `os.makedirs(..., exist_ok=True)` at line 34 can raise too. — **Fix:** wrap the `makedirs`+`open` in try/except and either return `None` (caller already prints FATAL + exits) or raise a typed error main() catches; at minimum document that acquire_lock can raise on an unwritable path so the caller isn't surprised.

**[nit] backend/utils/single_instance.py:20,26 — unparameterized `IO` generic** (`Optional[IO]`). mypy is reported clean (bare `IO` defaults to `IO[Any]`), so this is cosmetic, but `IO[str]` is the precise type for an `"a+"`/text-mode handle. — **Fix (optional):** annotate `Optional[IO[str]]`.

**[nit] backend/utils/single_instance.py:51 — PID written without a trailing newline / encoding is platform-default.** `fh.write(str(os.getpid()))` is best-effort diagnostic only (the comment says so) and the test reads it back with `.strip()`, so harmless. Mentioned only for completeness; not worth changing.

### Verified clean (no findings)
- **Race prevention / ordering** — lock acquired at main.py:743, `None`→`sys.exit(1)` at 744-748, **before** `Database()` (750) and `fail_stale_jobs()` (754). The destructive sweep cannot run until the lock is held. Correct.
- **fd kept alive** — `_instance_lock` is a module global (main.py:148), assigned under `global _instance_lock` (742); never GC'd → lock held for process lifetime. Correct.
- **OS release on crash/exit** — `fcntl.flock` (POSIX, line 43) and `msvcrt.locking` byte-range lock (Windows, line 40) are both kernel-managed and released when the handle closes or the process dies. The docstring's "no stale-lock problem" claim is accurate.
- **CLI-mode implication** — lock is intentionally before the mode branch (738-748 precede the `if args.video_path and not args.server` at 759), so CLI single-shot *and* `--server` both lock. A CLI run while a server is up correctly refuses (it would otherwise sweep the server's jobs). No `release_lock` on the CLI return paths, but OS-on-exit covers it. **No stale lock file on disk matters** — the lock is the open-handle, not file contents; the leftover `.lock` file is harmless and re-lockable.
- **Different output-dirs coexist** — lock path is `db_path + ".lock"` (743) and `db_path` = `join(output_dir, db_name)` (736); distinct output-dirs → distinct lock paths → no conflict. Covered by test_single_instance.py:30 and the integration test (test_server_entrypoint.py:136 uses same dir to prove refusal).
- **msvcrt write-after-lock** — locks 1 byte at offset 0 (40), then `seek(0)/truncate()/write` (49-51) from the *owning* handle; Windows byte-range locks block *other* handles, not the owner, so the write succeeds. Correct.
- **db_path is always local** — it is `join(local output_dir, db_name)`; the `gs://`/`gcs://` URI handling applies only to the *input video* (`storage.resolve_input_ref`, 764-773), never to `db_path`. The lock never sees a remote URI.
- **Lock file ≠ SQLite DB file** — separate paths: `sports_indexer.db` vs `sports_indexer.db.lock`. Holding the `.lock` handle does not lock the DB file. Confirmed.
- **sys.platform branching** — uses `sys.platform == "win32"` (correct for mypy platform-narrowing; the docstring at 22-23 explains why not `os.name`); imports `msvcrt`/`fcntl` inside the matching branch only, so neither cross-errors. Correct.
- **TOCTOU** — none: acquisition is a single atomic non-blocking lock syscall (`LOCK_NB` / `LK_NBLCK`); there is no check-then-act gap.

### Verdict
Functionally correct and the race is genuinely closed; the only real (minor) issue is that an unwritable lock path raises out of `acquire_lock` instead of degrading to the clean FATAL refusal — worth a try/except wrap, but not a merge blocker.

### 385. assistant (2026-06-21T17:10:03.049Z)

Pulled `khelsutra` (up to date, now at #67). Here's exactly what "touches the separate khelsutra repo" means, grounded in what I just verified:

## The two-repo split
- **The engine** = *this* repo, `Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer` — the Python/FastAPI backend and the wheel I've been hardening.
- **The actual UI you click** (per-rally grid, query bar, build→download, en/kn/hi) = a **different git repo**, `avidullu/khelsutra`, in its `web/` folder (Vite/React/TS). Different repo — different GitHub namespace even (`avidullu/` vs `Khelsutra/`), Cloudflare-connected, actively developed by the sibling sessions.
- What the engine serves today at `/` is a **legacy dead mini-dashboard** (`backend/api/static/{index.html,app.js,style.css}`), **not** that SPA.

## Why P1 unavoidably reaches across repos
Right now those run as **two processes** in dev — the Vite dev server proxies `/api` → engine:8000. But a *batteries-included download* must be **one thing**: the engine serves the real UI itself, single-origin (the SPA already calls same-origin `/api`, so no SPA code change — confirmed). To do that, the **engine wheel has to physically contain the built UI**.

The catch: the SPA's build output (`web/dist/`) is **gitignored in khelsutra** — it's produced by `npm run build`, committed nowhere. And we can't run `npm` on the user's machine at `pip install` time. So **at our package-build time, the SPA must already be built and copied into the engine tree** (e.g. `backend/ui/`, force-included into the wheel). That build-time dependency on a second, independently-developed repo *is* the cross-repo coupling. It's not something the engine repo can decide entirely on its own — and I don't want to unilaterally restructure your product/brand repo or its Cloudflare wiring.

So P1 splits cleanly:
- **P1a — engine-only, fully unblocked**: the 3 net-new endpoints (`/api/capabilities`, `/api/videos` list, `/api/reels`) + an `/api/version` handshake + contract test. No khelsutra involvement. **I'll start here now.**
- **P1b — the cross-repo bundling**: needs your call on *how* the SPA gets into the wheel.

That one decision is genuinely yours (it touches repo structure + your separate product repo), so:

    def add_video(self, video_id: str, filepath: str, status: str, validation_results: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:
        """Register or update a video row WITHOUT touching its child segments.

        Uses UPSERT, not INSERT OR REPLACE: REPLACE deletes the conflicting row, which —
        with foreign_keys ON — cascade-deletes every play_segment/event/candidate_segment.
        That made a status update (e.g. marking 'failed' on re-validation, or 'processed'
        before the segmenter runs) silently destroy prior good results. UPSERT mutates the
        row in place; clearing segments is now an explicit, deliberate operation
        (clear_video_data / prune_segments).
        """
        return self._execute_write(
            "INSERT INTO videos (id, filepath, status, validation_results) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?) "
            "ON CONFLICT(id) DO UPDATE SET "
            "filepath=excluded.filepath, status=excluded.status, "
            "validation_results=excluded.validation_results",
            (video_id, filepath, status, json.dumps(validation_results)),
            "Failed to add video",
        )

### 414. user (2026-06-21T17:24:35.424Z)

Path safety holds empirically:
- `..%2f..%2f...` (encoded `/`) → Starlette decodes `%2f` to `/` inside the single-segment param, then `safe_output_filename` would reject it — but here it 404s (the decoded `../../backend/main.py` doesn't match a `.mp4`/`.mov` and isn't a file in output_dir; the path containment + ext gate catch it). Either way, no escape.
- Null byte → 400 (rejected by `safe_output_filename`).
- Backslash → 400 (rejected).

Traversal is not reachable. Q2 is clean.

I have enough evidence for all six review axes. Here are my findings.

---

## Findings

**[minor] backend/main.py:49 and backend/main.py:261 — The inline comments `# cheap env-based GPU probe (no torch import)` and `cuda = probe_cuda_available()  # cheap, env-based (no torch import)` are factually false.** `probe_cuda_available()` → `presets.py:106 _cuda_available()` → `import torch; torch.cuda.is_available()` (presets.py:90-92). Measured: first `/api/capabilities` call takes ~3.5s (torch import + CUDA init); subsequent calls are cached (0ms). The function is correctly wrapped in try/except so it never raises and returns the right bool, but it is NOT cheap and NOT env-based on the first call. **Fix:** correct both comments to "best-effort CUDA probe (lazy torch import, ~3s first call, cached after; never raises)", or, if first-run-wizard latency matters, gate the torch probe behind a query param / cache it at startup.

**[minor] backend/infrastructure/database.py:480 — `list_videos` docstring claims "the videos table has no timestamp, so order by rowid", but the table DOES have `processed_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP` (database.py:121).** The ordering choice itself is *correct and arguably better* — `id` is `TEXT PRIMARY KEY` so `rowid` is a true monotonic insertion counter, immune to the 1-second granularity ties `processed_at` would suffer. Only the stated rationale is wrong. **Fix:** reword to "order by rowid (monotonic insertion order; more reliable than the 1s-granularity processed_at for newest-first)".

**[nit] backend/main.py:269 — `/api/capabilities` serializes full `ffmpeg_path` / `ffprobe_path` on an unauthenticated endpoint, disclosing the install layout (and OS username on Windows paths).** This is *consistent* with the established posture of the sibling localhost endpoints — `/api/videos` returns `filepath` (full source paths) and `/api/config` returns `output.output_dir` — so it is an acceptable disclosure under the current single-user-localhost threat model, not a regression. No secret value or full secret path is leaked: `gemini_key_present` is `bool(os.environ.get("GEMINI_API_KEY"))` and `wasb_weights_present` is `bool(weights_path) and os.path.exists(weights_path)` — both pure bools, neither the key nor the weights path crosses the wire (verified by the test asserting `"AIza-should-not-leak" not in r.text`). **Fix (optional):** if exposure tightens later, drop `ffmpeg_path`/`ffprobe_path` to bools-only like the secret fields; fine to defer.

### Axes that are clean (no issue)
- **Q1 SECRET LEAK:** Clean. Only booleans for key/weights presence; no key value, no full secret path. (`ffmpeg_path` disclosure noted above as a consistent nit.)
- **Q2 PATH SAFETY:** Clean. `safe_output_filename` rejects empty/null-byte/`/`/`\\`/absolute/`..`/non-basename; `resolve_under_root` (realpath + commonpath) is real defense-in-depth catching symlink escape; the `.mp4`/`.mov` ext gate is ANDed with `os.path.isfile`. Route is single-segment `{filename}` (not `{filename:path}`) so separators don't span. Empirically probed encoded-`/`, encoded-`..`, null-byte, backslash → all 400/404, no escape. `os.scandir` handle is closed via `with entries:` (main.py:316). No exploitable TOCTOU (realpath resolved, then `isfile`, then `FileResponse` opens — worst case is a 404/serve of a since-replaced file under the same contained root, not an escape).
- **Q3 HONESTY:** Accurate. Confirmed `/api/compile` → `VideoCompiler(output_dir).compile_highlights` writes flat to `os.path.join(self.output_dir, os.path.basename(name))` (compiler.py:326) with no `video_id` association — the "global flat list / per-video would be a phantom" docstring is honest. `e.is_file()` correctly skips the per-video `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(dir=output_dir)` scratch subdirs (compiler.py:340), and the SQLite DB / `.lock` file are excluded by the `.mp4`/`.mov` ext gate regardless of location. Test covers `notes.txt` + a subdir exclusion.
- **Q4 /api/videos:** Clean. SQL parameterized — `LIMIT ?` with `int(limit)` coercion (database.py:485-487), no injection. `SELECT *` + `validation_results` JSON-parse mirrors `get_video` exactly, so it leaks nothing the existing per-row endpoint didn't already expose. `ORDER BY rowid DESC` is sound (see minor above).
- **Q5 ROBUSTNESS:** Holds the "never raises" contract. All config access is None-safe `getattr` chains (verified `global_config=None` path), `segmenter_registry.list_available()` is per-item try/excepted and returns `{}` when empty, `probe_cuda_available()` is try/excepted, `shutil.which`/`os.path.exists("")` don't raise. (The torch-import *cost*, not correctness, is the only issue — see minor.)
- **Q6 /api/version:** Correct. `_engine_version()` catches `PackageNotFoundError` → `"unknown"` for editable/source runs (main.py:240-244). `API_VERSION = 1` contract comment ("BUMP when an existing endpoint's shape changes incompatibly; additive endpoints need no bump") is accurate and matches the additive nature of this PR.

### Verdict
Clean — no blockers or majors; two minor doc/comment-accuracy fixes (the false "no torch import" comments at main.py:49/261 are the most worth correcting since they misrepresent a real ~3.5s first-call cost) and one optional nit. Safe to merge after the comment fix.

### 443. assistant (2026-06-21T17:35:12.384Z)

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Bundle the KhelSutra web SPA into the engine so a downloaded engine serves the real UI
# single-origin (PACKAGING_PLAN.md P1b).
#
# OPTION 1 (now, owner-chosen): build the SPA from a khelsutra checkout and vendor the built `dist/`
# into `backend/ui/`, which is COMMITTED — so the wheel + CI build fully offline/reproducibly. Refresh
# the snapshot by re-running this on an SPA release; commit the diff.
#
# OPTION 3 (designed-for, future): point --from at a published dist tarball/URL (a GitHub Release
# artifact) to vendor a pre-built SPA without a local khelsutra checkout or Node. Already wired below.
#
# Usage:
#   scripts/bundle_ui.sh [SOURCE]
#     SOURCE = a khelsutra checkout dir (default: $KHELSUTRA_DIR or ../khelsutra)   [builds it]
#            | a path/URL ending .tgz/.tar.gz                                       [unpacks it]
#   BUILD=0 scripts/bundle_ui.sh <khelsutra-dir>   # skip npm; reuse an existing web/dist
set -euo pipefail

ENGINE_ROOT="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
UI_DEST="$ENGINE_ROOT/backend/ui"
SOURCE="${1:-${KHELSUTRA_DIR:-$ENGINE_ROOT/../khelsutra}}"

stage_dist() {  # populates $1 (a dist dir) into a clean $UI_DEST
  local dist="$1"
  [ -f "$dist/index.html" ] || { echo "ERROR: no index.html in $dist — not a built SPA dist" >&2; exit 1; }
  rm -rf "$UI_DEST"
  mkdir -p "$UI_DEST"
  cp -r "$dist"/. "$UI_DEST"/
}

SPA_REPO="unknown"
SPA_COMMIT="unknown"

case "$SOURCE" in
  *.tgz|*.tar.gz|http://*|https://*)
    # OPTION 3: a published dist artifact.
    tmp="$(mktemp -d)"; trap 'rm -rf "$tmp"' EXIT
    if [[ "$SOURCE" == http*://* ]]; then
      echo "[bundle_ui] fetching dist artifact: $SOURCE"
      curl -fsSL "$SOURCE" -o "$tmp/dist.tgz"; SRC_TGZ="$tmp/dist.tgz"; SPA_REPO="$SOURCE"
    else
      SRC_TGZ="$SOURCE"; SPA_REPO="$SOURCE"
    fi
    tar -xzf "$SRC_TGZ" -C "$tmp"
    # accept either dist/* at the root or a top-level dir containing index.html
    if [ -f "$tmp/index.html" ]; then DIST="$tmp"; else DIST="$(dirname "$(find "$tmp" -name index.html | head -1)")"; fi
    stage_dist "$DIST"
    ;;
  *)
    # OPTION 1: a khelsutra checkout — build it.
    WEB="$SOURCE/web"
    [ -d "$WEB" ] || { echo "ERROR: $WEB not found (pass a khelsutra checkout dir or a dist tarball)" >&2; exit 1; }
    SPA_REPO="$(git -C "$SOURCE" config --get remote.origin.url 2>/dev/null || echo "$SOURCE")"
    SPA_COMMIT="$(git -C "$SOURCE" rev-parse HEAD 2>/dev/null || echo unknown)"
    if [ "${BUILD:-1}" = "1" ]; then
      echo "[bundle_ui] building SPA in $WEB (npm ci && npm run build)"
      ( cd "$WEB" && npm ci && npm run build )
    else
      echo "[bundle_ui] BUILD=0 — reusing existing $WEB/dist"
    fi
    stage_dist "$WEB/dist"
    ;;
esac

# Provenance + the SPA<->engine contract version (the engine's current API_VERSION at bundle time).
ENGINE_API_VERSION="$(grep -E '^API_VERSION\s*=' "$ENGINE_ROOT/backend/main.py" | grep -oE '[0-9]+' | head -1)"
CONTENT_HASH="$(find "$UI_DEST" -type f ! -name ui_version.json | LC_ALL=C sort | xargs sha256sum | sha256sum | cut -d' ' -f1)"
BUILT_AT="$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ 2>/dev/null || echo unknown)"

cat > "$UI_DEST/ui_version.json" <<JSON
{
  "spa_repo": "$SPA_REPO",
  "spa_commit": "$SPA_COMMIT",
  "engine_api_version": ${ENGINE_API_VERSION:-null},
  "content_hash": "$CONTENT_HASH",
  "built_at": "$BUILT_AT"
}
JSON

echo "[bundle_ui] vendored SPA -> $UI_DEST (spa_commit=$SPA_COMMIT, engine_api_version=$ENGINE_API_VERSION)"
echo "[bundle_ui] files:"; find "$UI_DEST" -type f | sed "s|$UI_DEST/||" | sort

### 456. assistant (2026-06-21T17:37:10.691Z)

"""The bundled SPA is served single-origin + the SPA<->engine contract (PACKAGING_PLAN.md P1b)."""

import json
import os

import pytest

pytest.importorskip("httpx")
from fastapi.testclient import TestClient

import backend.main as m

client = TestClient(m.app)
UI_DIR = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(m.__file__)), "ui")


def test_bundled_ui_snapshot_is_committed():
    assert os.path.isfile(os.path.join(UI_DIR, "index.html")), (
        "backend/ui/index.html missing — the SPA snapshot must be committed (run scripts/bundle_ui.sh).")
    assert os.path.isfile(os.path.join(UI_DIR, "ui_version.json"))


def test_serves_spa_at_root():
    r = client.get("/")
    assert r.status_code == 200
    # The bundled index.html references its single-origin /assets bundle (Vite base='/').
    assert "/assets/" in r.text


def test_serves_spa_assets_byte_for_byte():
    assets = os.path.join(UI_DIR, "assets")
    js = next(f for f in os.listdir(assets) if f.endswith(".js"))
    r = client.get(f"/assets/{js}")
    assert r.status_code == 200
    with open(os.path.join(assets, js), "rb") as f:
        assert r.content == f.read()  # served verbatim (mimetype varies by OS, so compare bytes)


def test_api_version_reports_bundled_ui():
    body = client.get("/api/version").json()
    assert body["api_version"] == m.API_VERSION
    assert body["ui"] is not None
    assert body["ui"]["spa_commit"]  # provenance recorded


def test_spa_engine_contract_version_matches():
    """Skew guard: the committed snapshot records the engine API_VERSION it was built against. If
    someone bumps API_VERSION without re-running scripts/bundle_ui.sh, this FAILS — forcing a
    re-bundle so the shipped SPA can't silently target a stale API contract."""
    with open(os.path.join(UI_DIR, "ui_version.json"), encoding="utf-8") as f:
        uiv = json.load(f)
    assert uiv["engine_api_version"] == m.API_VERSION, (
        f"bundled SPA built against engine api_version {uiv['engine_api_version']} but engine is now "
        f"{m.API_VERSION} — re-run scripts/bundle_ui.sh and commit backend/ui/.")

## Adversarial review — PR P1b (bundle khelsutra SPA into engine wheel, single-origin)

**[nit] backend/main.py:67 / scripts/bundle_ui.sh:67 — `content_hash` is path-dependent, not a reproducible content fingerprint** — `CONTENT_HASH="$(find "$UI_DEST" ... | xargs sha256sum | sha256sum ...)"` runs `find` over the *absolute* `$UI_DEST` path, and the outer `sha256sum` hashes a stream that embeds those absolute paths (verified: it reproduces `fad6253…` only with the literal `/c/Users/avidu/Projects/bhi-wt-p1b/...` prefix; a relative-path recompute gives `ac2a3d6…`). So re-bundling identical bytes from any other checkout path/machine/CI yields a different hash. It's recorded as provenance but isn't a verifiable fingerprint, and no test asserts it. — Fix: hash with paths relativized to `$UI_DEST` (e.g. `cd "$UI_DEST" && find . -type f ! -name ui_version.json | LC_ALL=C sort | xargs sha256sum | sha256sum`), or drop the field if it's only decorative.

**[nit] scripts/bundle_ui.sh:66 — `API_VERSION` extraction is brittle to a type annotation** — the anchor `^API_VERSION\s*=` matches today's `API_VERSION = 1` (verified → `1`), but a future `API_VERSION: int = 1` would not match → `ENGINE_API_VERSION` empty → json written as `engine_api_version: null`. This fails *safe* (the skew test then asserts `null == 1` and fails loudly, prompting a re-bundle), so it's not a guard hole — just a footgun. — Fix: loosen to `^API_VERSION\b[^=]*=` or note the assumption.

**[minor] backend/ui/assets/*.js + *.woff2 — minified third-party JS (React etc.) + OFL Noto fonts are committed into the MIT engine repo, and the license-scan lane does not cover them** — `.github/workflows/ci.yml:142` runs `pip-licenses` (Python-only); it cannot see the JS/font blobs. No `LICENSE`/`NOTICE` is vendored alongside the bundle (verified: none under `backend/ui/`). Given the project's hard MIT/Apache/BSD-only guardrail for *all* deps, and that OFL/MIT carry attribution obligations, this warrants an explicit note in the PR (and ideally a vendored third-party-notices file from the SPA build). Not a serving/correctness blocker. — Fix: PR should NOTE the JS/font licensing posture; consider vendoring the SPA's generated licenses file into `backend/ui/`.

**Verified clean (no issue):**
- **Serving correctness** — `serve_index` (main.py:151) reads `_UI_DIR/index.html`; `/assets` mount (main.py:147) is guarded by `os.path.isdir(_UI_ASSETS_DIR)` (146), registered at import only when the dir exists. No `/assets`-prefixed API route exists → no conflict; `/static` (136) is a distinct prefix. Fallback when `backend/ui` absent: `_UI_DIR` → `_STATIC_DIR` (144), legacy `index.html` references `/static/*` served by the always-present `/static` mount (verified functional, no crash); `_UI_ASSETS_DIR` becomes a non-existent `static/assets` so `/assets` is simply not mounted. Traversal: Starlette 1.0.0 `StaticFiles` normalizes and 404s `../` escapes.
- **Route order / shadowing** — no `{path:path}` catch-all anywhere (main.py + `backend/api/uploads.py` router routes are all `/api/upload/*`). Serving `index.html` at `/` (150) does not intercept `/api/*` or `/static`.
- **Bundler robustness** — `set -euo pipefail` (sh:17); option-3 uses `curl -fsSL` + `tar -xzf` into a `mktemp -d` with `trap … EXIT` cleanup; `stage_dist` validates `index.html` presence and does `rm -rf` + `mkdir -p` + `cp -r "$dist"/.` into a clean dest (correct). Untrusted-tarball traversal is the standard `tar` caveat but the source is the owner's own release artifact (acceptable). CI never bundles — no `.github/` reference to `bundle_ui`/`npm`/`backend/ui` (verified); the snapshot is committed.
- **Skew guard** — genuine: `test_spa_engine_contract_version_matches` (test_ui_bundle.py:46) loads the committed `ui_version.json` and asserts `engine_api_version == m.API_VERSION`; bumping `API_VERSION` without re-bundling makes them differ → FAIL. No spurious-pass path.
- **CRLF/binary** — `.gitattributes:15 backend/ui/** -text` resolves to `text: unset` for `.html/.js/.json/.woff2` (verified via `git check-attr`); repo has `core.autocrlf=true`, so this rule is *necessary* and correct — committed bytes will match Vite output. `ui_version.json` is also covered by `-text` (it's generated, never hand-edited, so no LF normalization needed — fine).
- **/api/version robustness** — `_bundled_ui_version` (main.py:159) reads `_BUNDLED_UI_DIR/ui_version.json` and returns `None` on `(OSError, ValueError)` (covers missing file + parse error); `json` imported (main.py:4), `Optional/Dict/Any` imported (main.py:32). `/api/version` still 200s without a bundle.
- **Wheel packaging** — built the wheel from the current (untracked `backend/ui/`) tree: all 10 UI files including the woff2 fonts are present (`backend/ui/...`). No force-include needed; claim holds.
- **Targeted tests** — `tests/test_ui_bundle.py`: 5 passed.

### 508. assistant (2026-06-21T18:01:21.957Z)

"""The friendly `indexer --app` launcher (PACKAGING_PLAN.md P2)."""

import os
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

import pytest

import backend.main as m

REPO_ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))


def _free_port() -> int:
    s = socket.socket()
    s.bind(("127.0.0.1", 0))
    p = int(s.getsockname()[1])
    s.close()
    return p


# ---- port helpers ----

def test_find_and_check_free_port():
    p = m._find_free_port("127.0.0.1")
    assert isinstance(p, int) and p > 0
    assert m._port_is_free("127.0.0.1", p) is True


def test_resolve_app_port_keeps_free_port():
    p = _free_port()
    assert m._resolve_app_port("127.0.0.1", p) == p


def test_resolve_app_port_falls_back_when_busy():
    s = socket.socket()
    s.bind(("127.0.0.1", 0))
    s.listen()
    busy = int(s.getsockname()[1])
    try:
        assert m._port_is_free("127.0.0.1", busy) is False
        alt = m._resolve_app_port("127.0.0.1", busy)
        assert alt != busy
        assert m._port_is_free("127.0.0.1", alt) is True
    finally:
        s.close()


# ---- open-browser-when-ready ----

class _FakeResp:
    status = 200
    def __enter__(self): return self
    def __exit__(self, *a): return False


def test_open_browser_opens_once_healthy(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.delenv("RALLY_NO_BROWSER", raising=False)
    monkeypatch.setattr(urllib.request, "urlopen", lambda *a, **k: _FakeResp())
    opened = {}
    monkeypatch.setattr(m.webbrowser, "open", lambda u: opened.setdefault("url", u) or True)
    assert m._open_browser_when_ready("http://127.0.0.1:1/", "http://127.0.0.1:1/api/health", timeout=2) is True
    assert opened["url"] == "http://127.0.0.1:1/"


def test_open_browser_suppressed_by_env(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setenv("RALLY_NO_BROWSER", "1")
    monkeypatch.setattr(m.webbrowser, "open", lambda u: pytest.fail("must not open when suppressed"))
    assert m._open_browser_when_ready("http://x/", "http://x/api/health") is False


def test_open_browser_gives_up_when_never_ready(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.delenv("RALLY_NO_BROWSER", raising=False)

    def _boom(*a, **k):
        raise OSError("connection refused")

    monkeypatch.setattr(urllib.request, "urlopen", _boom)
    monkeypatch.setattr(m.webbrowser, "open", lambda u: pytest.fail("must not open a dead page"))
    assert m._open_browser_when_ready("http://x/", "http://x/api/health", timeout=0.4) is False


# ---- end-to-end: `indexer --app` boots the server + serves the bundled UI ----

def test_app_mode_boots_and_serves_bundled_ui(tmp_path):
    """`indexer --app` (browser suppressed) must start the server localhost-only and serve both
    /api/health and the bundled SPA at /."""
    port = _free_port()
    env = {
        **os.environ,
        "RALLY_NO_BROWSER": "1",  # don't pop a browser in CI
        "PYTHONPATH": REPO_ROOT + os.pathsep + os.environ.get("PYTHONPATH", ""),
    }
    log = open(tmp_path / "app.log", "wb")
    proc = subprocess.Popen(
        [sys.executable, "-m", "backend.main", "--app", "--port", str(port),
         "--output-dir", str(tmp_path / "out")],
        cwd=REPO_ROOT, env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
    )
    base = f"http://127.0.0.1:{port}"
    try:
        deadline = time.time() + 90
        healthy = False
        while time.time() < deadline:
            if proc.poll() is not None:
                pytest.fail(f"--app server exited early (code {proc.returncode}); see {tmp_path/'app.log'}")
            try:
                with urllib.request.urlopen(f"{base}/api/health", timeout=2) as r:
                    if r.status == 200:
                        healthy = True
                        break
            except (urllib.error.URLError, ConnectionError, OSError):
                time.sleep(0.5)
        assert healthy, "the --app server never became healthy"
        with urllib.request.urlopen(f"{base}/", timeout=5) as r:
            body = r.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
        assert "/assets/" in body  # the bundled SPA index.html (single-origin asset refs)
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        try:
            proc.wait(timeout=10)
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            proc.kill()
        log.close()

### 514. user (2026-06-21T18:06:41.665Z)

All confirmed. The health endpoint is auth-free and the poll uses `Host: 127.0.0.1:<port>` which passes the TrustedHostMiddleware default allowlist. Note one interaction worth flagging: if a user sets `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` without including `127.0.0.1`, the health poll would 400 — but the default keeps `127.0.0.1`, and `--app` forces 127.0.0.1 bind, so the default path is safe. Setting `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` to an exclusive non-loopback set while using `--app` is a self-inflicted misconfiguration; the health poll would then never see 200 and the browser simply wouldn't open (no dead page). Acceptable.

Here are my findings.

---

**Adversarial review — PR P2b (`indexer --app` launcher)**

**1. Mode interactions** — Clean. The CLI single-shot guard at `backend/main.py:970` (`if args.video_path and not args.server and not args.app`) correctly excludes `--app`, so `--video-path --app` never runs single-shot processing and falls through to the server block at `:1051` (`if args.app or args.server or not args.video_path`). `--app --server` → `--app` branch wins (`:1052` checks `args.app` first) → localhost-only, which is the safe precedence. No-args → server (`not args.video_path`). No combination runs two modes. The `--setup`/`--trim`/`--debug-clip` branches (`:872`,`:886`,`:918`) return early and require their own flags, independent of `--app`.

**2. Localhost invariant** — Confirmed. `:1055` hardcodes `bind_host = "127.0.0.1"` for `--app`, fully bypassing `_resolve_bind_host` / `--host` / `RALLY_BIND_HOST` / `edge_auth_enabled`. `--server` still honors the P0c profile via `_resolve_bind_host(global_config, args.host)` at `:1065`. Intended and safe — the friendly app can never expose the network.

**3. Browser-open thread** — Solid. Daemon thread (`:1062-1063` `daemon=True`) → won't block process exit or Ctrl+C. `RALLY_NO_BROWSER` honored at `:158`. Dead-page guard: the `while/else` at `:163-171` returns False without opening if health never returns 200 (no open on a server that fails to bind). `webbrowser.open` is wrapped in try/except (`:172-175`), exceptions swallowed. No race: it polls `/api/health` and only opens after a 200. Health URL is `url + "api/health"` = `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/health` (url ends in `/`, `:1059`) — `urllib` sends `Host: 127.0.0.1:<port>`, which passes TrustedHostMiddleware (port stripped → `127.0.0.1`, in the default allowlist at `:100`). `/api/health` is auth-free (`:298`).

**4. Free-port (TOCTOU)** — Acceptable as documented. `_resolve_app_port` (`:149-151`) checks `_port_is_free` then uvicorn binds later. `_port_is_free` (`:131`) binds without `SO_REUSEADDR`; uvicorn binds with `SO_REUSEADDR=1` — a strictly-free port passes both, so the check is not falsely positive. `_find_free_port` (`:142`) returns an OS-assigned ephemeral port (free at that instant). Window is best-effort; uvicorn surfaces a real bind failure loudly (docstring `:132-133`). The resolved `port` is used consistently for BOTH the browser URL (`:1059`) and `uvicorn.run` (`:1077`).

**5. Single-instance (P0e)** — Correct. `--app` reaches `acquire_lock(db_path + ".lock")` at `:946` and `fail_stale_jobs()` at `:965` (no early return between `:924` and the lock — verified). A 2nd `--app` on the same `--output-dir` → lock fails → `[FATAL]` + `sys.exit(1)` at `:956-959`. Lock-infra failure (read-only dir) warns and proceeds without the guard (`:951`), not a crash.

**6. Resource/socket leaks** — None. Both `_port_is_free` (`:134`) and `_find_free_port` (`:144`) use `with socket.socket(...)` → sockets always closed. `urllib.request.urlopen` uses `with` (`:165`). `webbrowser.open` exceptions swallowed (`:174`).

**[nit] backend/main.py:95-100** — If a user sets `RALLY_ALLOWED_HOSTS` to an exclusively non-loopback set *and* uses `--app`, the health poll's `Host: 127.0.0.1` would 400 and the browser would silently never open (no dead page, no crash). Self-inflicted misconfiguration and the default path is safe; worth a one-line note that `--app` always needs loopback in the allowlist, but not blocking.

**Verdict:** Clean — no blocker/major/minor issues; one nit. Ship it.

### 532. assistant (2026-06-21T18:20:56.852Z)

"""Single resolver for the ffmpeg / ffprobe binaries (PACKAGING_PLAN.md P2a).

Every ffmpeg/ffprobe invocation in the engine goes through :func:`ffmpeg_bin` / :func:`ffprobe_bin`
so a packaged app can point at a vendored or non-PATH binary via the ``RALLY_FFMPEG`` /
``RALLY_FFPROBE`` env vars, there is ONE place to add a config knob or a louder check later, and a
missing ffmpeg fails with an actionable, OS-specific install hint instead of a cryptic
``FileNotFoundError`` from deep in the pipeline.

DEFAULT (env unset) returns the bare name (``"ffmpeg"`` / ``"ffprobe"``) → PATH resolution, exactly
the prior hardcoded literals (byte-identical argv), so this is a pure seam with no behaviour change.
stdlib-only.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import os
import platform
import shutil


class FfmpegNotFound(RuntimeError):
    """Raised by :func:`require_ffmpeg` when ffmpeg/ffprobe can't be located."""


def ffmpeg_bin() -> str:
    """The ffmpeg executable: ``RALLY_FFMPEG`` if set, else ``"ffmpeg"`` (resolved on PATH)."""
    return (os.environ.get("RALLY_FFMPEG") or "").strip() or "ffmpeg"


def ffprobe_bin() -> str:
    """The ffprobe executable: ``RALLY_FFPROBE`` if set, else ``"ffprobe"`` (resolved on PATH)."""
    return (os.environ.get("RALLY_FFPROBE") or "").strip() or "ffprobe"


def ffmpeg_available() -> bool:
    return shutil.which(ffmpeg_bin()) is not None


def ffprobe_available() -> bool:
    return shutil.which(ffprobe_bin()) is not None


def install_hint() -> str:
    """The OS-appropriate one-line install command (mirrors scripts/doctor.py)."""
    system = platform.system()
    if system == "Windows":
        return "winget install Gyan.FFmpeg"
    if system == "Darwin":
        return "brew install ffmpeg"
    return "sudo apt-get install -y ffmpeg   (or your distro's package manager)"


def require_ffmpeg() -> None:
    """Raise :class:`FfmpegNotFound` (with an OS-specific install hint) if ffmpeg/ffprobe is missing.

    A loud, actionable pre-flight for entry points that are about to shell out — used by the
    first-run self-test / wizard; the per-call-site argv still goes through :func:`ffmpeg_bin`."""
    missing = [name for name, ok in (("ffmpeg", ffmpeg_available()), ("ffprobe", ffprobe_available())) if not ok]
    if missing:
        raise FfmpegNotFound(
            f"{' and '.join(missing)} not found (PATH or RALLY_FFMPEG/RALLY_FFPROBE). FFmpeg is "
            f"required for video processing. Install it:  {install_hint()}")

7	def get_video_duration(video_path: str) -> float:
8	    """Returns the exact duration of a video file in seconds using ffprobe.
9	    Returns 0.0 on failure."""
10	    try:
11	        duration_output = subprocess.check_output([
12	            "ffprobe", "-v", "error", "-show_entries", "format=duration",
13	            "-of", "default=noprint_wrappers=1:nokey=1", video_path
14	        ]).decode().strip()
15	        return float(duration_output)
16	    except Exception as e:
17	        logger.error(f"Could not detect video duration for {video_path}: {e}")
18	        return 0.0
19	
20	def extract_video_chunk(video_path: str, start_time: float, duration: float,
21	                        output_path: str, reencode: bool = False,
22	                        scale_width: Optional[int] = None) -> bool:
23	    """Extracts a chunk from a video file.
24	
25	    Args:
26	        reencode: If True, re-encodes with libx264 (slower but frame-accurate).
27	                  If False, uses stream-copy (fast but cuts at nearest keyframe).
28	                  Use True for short clips where keyframe drift matters.
29	        scale_width: If set (requires reencode), downscale to this width (height auto,
30	                  even). Useful for high-res sources headed to an API that analyses at
31	                  low res anyway — shrinks uploads with no analysis loss.
32	    """
33	    cmd = [
34	        "ffmpeg", "-y",
35	        "-ss", str(start_time),
36	        "-t", str(duration),
37	        "-i", video_path,
38	    ]
39	    if reencode:
40	        if scale_width:
41	            cmd += ["-vf", f"scale={int(scale_width)}:-2"]
42	        cmd += ["-c:v", "libx264", "-preset", "ultrafast", "-crf", "23", "-c:a", "aac"]
43	    else:
44	        cmd += ["-c", "copy"]
45	    cmd.append(output_path)
46	    try:
47	        subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
48	        return True
49	    except Exception as e:
50	        logger.error(f"Failed to extract chunk {start_time}s - {start_time+duration}s: {e}")
51	        return False

"""The single ffmpeg/ffprobe resolver (PACKAGING_PLAN.md P2a)."""

import subprocess

import pytest

from backend.utils import ffmpeg as ff


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def _clear_ffmpeg_env(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.delenv("RALLY_FFMPEG", raising=False)
    monkeypatch.delenv("RALLY_FFPROBE", raising=False)


# ---- default (env unset) is byte-identical to the old hardcoded literals ----

def test_default_bins_are_bare_names():
    assert ff.ffmpeg_bin() == "ffmpeg"
    assert ff.ffprobe_bin() == "ffprobe"


def test_env_override(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setenv("RALLY_FFMPEG", "/opt/ff/ffmpeg")
    monkeypatch.setenv("RALLY_FFPROBE", "/opt/ff/ffprobe")
    assert ff.ffmpeg_bin() == "/opt/ff/ffmpeg"
    assert ff.ffprobe_bin() == "/opt/ff/ffprobe"


def test_blank_env_falls_back_to_default(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setenv("RALLY_FFMPEG", "   ")
    assert ff.ffmpeg_bin() == "ffmpeg"


# ---- availability + require ----

def test_available_reflects_which(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(ff.shutil, "which", lambda name: "/usr/bin/" + name)
    assert ff.ffmpeg_available() is True and ff.ffprobe_available() is True
    monkeypatch.setattr(ff.shutil, "which", lambda name: None)
    assert ff.ffmpeg_available() is False and ff.ffprobe_available() is False


def test_require_ffmpeg_noop_when_present(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(ff.shutil, "which", lambda name: "/usr/bin/" + name)
    ff.require_ffmpeg()  # must not raise


def test_require_ffmpeg_raises_with_hint_when_missing(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(ff.shutil, "which", lambda name: None)
    with pytest.raises(ff.FfmpegNotFound) as exc:
        ff.require_ffmpeg()
    msg = str(exc.value)
    assert "ffmpeg" in msg and "ffprobe" in msg
    assert ff.install_hint() in msg  # actionable, OS-specific


@pytest.mark.parametrize("system,expect", [
    ("Windows", "winget"), ("Darwin", "brew"), ("Linux", "apt-get")])
def test_install_hint_per_os(monkeypatch, system, expect):
    monkeypatch.setattr(ff.platform, "system", lambda: system)
    assert expect in ff.install_hint()


# ---- a wrapped call site actually uses the resolved binary ----

def test_call_site_uses_resolved_binary(monkeypatch):
    from backend.utils import video
    monkeypatch.setenv("RALLY_FFPROBE", "/custom/ffprobe")
    captured = {}

    def _fake_check_output(argv, *a, **k):
        captured["argv"] = argv
        return b"12.5"

    monkeypatch.setattr(subprocess, "check_output", _fake_check_output)
    assert video.get_video_duration("x.mp4") == 12.5
    assert captured["argv"][0] == "/custom/ffprobe"  # the override flows through to argv[0]

    @staticmethod
    def _add_column_idempotent(cursor: sqlite3.Cursor, ddl: str) -> None:
        """Apply a bare ``ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN`` migration step re-runnably.

        A DDL ``ALTER`` auto-commits independently of the surrounding ``with conn:``
        transaction, so an interrupted v3/v4 block can leave the column added while
        ``user_version`` stays un-bumped — and the next open would re-run the same
        ``ALTER`` and raise ``OperationalError: duplicate column name``, bricking every
        future ``Database(...)`` construction (``_init_db`` runs in ``__init__``). Swallow
        ONLY that specific error so the ladder stays crash-safe, matching the re-runnable
        ``CREATE ... IF NOT EXISTS`` v1/v2/v5 blocks.
        """
        try:
            cursor.execute(ddl)
        except sqlite3.OperationalError as exc:
            if "duplicate column name" not in str(exc).lower():
                raise

    def _init_db(self):
        """Initializes tables for videos, play_segments, and events."""
        with self._connect() as conn:
            cursor = conn.cursor()

            self._create_base_tables(cursor)

            # Versioned migrations live here. PRAGMA user_version is the schema
            # contract — bump it for every change and add an ordered `if version < N`
            # block below. Tests assert the expected version, so accidental drift fails CI.
            version = cursor.execute("PRAGMA user_version").fetchone()[0]

            if version < 1:
                self._migrate_to_v1(cursor)

            if version < 2:
                self._migrate_to_v2(cursor)

            if version < 3:
                self._migrate_to_v3(cursor)

            if version < 4:
                self._migrate_to_v4(cursor)

            if version < 5:
                self._migrate_to_v5(cursor)

            if version < 6:
                self._migrate_to_v6(cursor)

            if version < 7:
                self._migrate_to_v7(cursor)
            # future: if version < 8: cursor.execute("ALTER TABLE ..."); PRAGMA user_version = 8

            conn.commit()

=== _connect (WAL?) + db_path ===

class Database:
    def __init__(self, db_path: str):
        self.db_path = db_path
        self._init_db()

    @contextmanager
    def _connect(self):
        """Transaction scope that also CLOSES the connection.

        ``with sqlite3.connect(...)`` alone is only a commit/rollback scope — it
        never closes, so every call leaked its connection to GC (lingering file
        handles on the WAL sidecars under Windows). Internal methods use this.
        """
        conn = self._get_connection()
        try:
            with conn:
                yield conn
        finally:
            conn.close()

    def _get_connection(self):
        # busy_timeout: wait (not fail) on a locked DB — needed once the async job model
        # adds concurrent writers (a swallowed 'database is locked' silently drops segments).
        conn = sqlite3.connect(self.db_path, timeout=30.0)
        conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        # Enforce ON DELETE CASCADE / SET NULL — SQLite leaves FKs off per-connection.
        conn.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys = ON")
        # WAL allows concurrent readers during a write; busy_timeout bounds lock waits.
        conn.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout = 30000")
        try:
            conn.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode = WAL")
        except sqlite3.OperationalError:
            pass  # e.g. a read-only/networked FS that can't do WAL — degrade gracefully
        return conn

"""DB upgrade-safety contract for an auto-updating downloadable app (PACKAGING_PLAN.md P2d)."""

import os
import sqlite3

import pytest

from backend.infrastructure.database import SCHEMA_VERSION, Database, SchemaTooNewError


def _user_version(path: str) -> int:
    conn = sqlite3.connect(path)
    try:
        return int(conn.execute("PRAGMA user_version").fetchone()[0])
    finally:
        conn.close()


def _set_user_version(path: str, v: int) -> None:
    conn = sqlite3.connect(path)
    try:
        conn.execute(f"PRAGMA user_version = {int(v)}")
        conn.commit()
    finally:
        conn.close()


def test_schema_version_matches_ladder(tmp_path):
    """Ties the SCHEMA_VERSION constant to the migration ladder: a freshly-built DB lands exactly at
    SCHEMA_VERSION. Adding a _migrate_to_vN without bumping the constant fails here."""
    p = str(tmp_path / "t.db")
    Database(p)
    assert _user_version(p) == SCHEMA_VERSION


def test_fresh_db_makes_no_backup(tmp_path):
    Database(str(tmp_path / "t.db"))
    assert not any(".bak" in f.name for f in tmp_path.iterdir())  # version 0 → nothing to back up


def test_reopen_current_db_no_backup_no_error(tmp_path):
    p = str(tmp_path / "t.db")
    Database(p)   # → SCHEMA_VERSION
    Database(p)   # reopen at current version → no migration runs
    assert not any(".bak" in f.name for f in tmp_path.iterdir())


def test_downgrade_guard_refuses_newer_db(tmp_path):
    p = str(tmp_path / "t.db")
    Database(p)
    _set_user_version(p, SCHEMA_VERSION + 5)  # a DB from a hypothetical newer app
    with pytest.raises(SchemaTooNewError):
        Database(p)


def test_backup_before_migrate_on_upgrade(tmp_path):
    p = str(tmp_path / "t.db")
    db = Database(p)
    db.add_video("vid1", "/a.mp4", "processed", [])   # data that must survive
    _set_user_version(p, 3)                            # simulate an older on-disk schema

    Database(p)  # reopen with current code → upgrades in place, backing up first
    backup = tmp_path / "t.db.bak-v3"
    assert backup.exists(), "an upgrade must snapshot the DB to <db>.bak-v<old> first"
    assert _user_version(p) == SCHEMA_VERSION          # upgraded
    assert _user_version(str(backup)) == 3             # the backup is the PRE-upgrade snapshot
    assert Database(p).get_video("vid1") is not None   # data preserved across the upgrade


def test_backup_database_export(tmp_path):
    p = str(tmp_path / "t.db")
    db = Database(p)
    db.add_video("vid1", "/a.mp4", "processed", [])
    dest = str(tmp_path / "export" / "my_data.db")
    assert db.backup_database(dest) == dest
    assert os.path.exists(dest)                        # parent dir created
    assert _user_version(dest) == SCHEMA_VERSION
    assert Database(dest).get_video("vid1") is not None  # the export is a usable DB

## Adversarial review: PR P2d (DB upgrade-safety)

**1. DOWNGRADE GUARD** — `SCHEMA_VERSION = 7` (database.py:18) matches the ladder's max (`_migrate_to_v7` / `PRAGMA user_version = 7` at database.py:395-402; no `_migrate_to_v8`). The pin test `test_schema_version_matches_ladder` (test_db_upgrade_safety.py:28) plus the existing `==7` tests enforce drift. **No false-positive risk** on a current/older DB: the guard is `version > SCHEMA_VERSION` (database.py:109), so a v7 DB (`7 > 7` false) and any older DB pass straight through. **No leaked handle on raise**: the guard raises *inside* `with self._connect() as conn:` (database.py:96), and `_connect`'s `finally: conn.close()` (database.py:42) runs during exception unwind — verified the connection is closed before `SchemaTooNewError` escapes `__init__`. Clean.

**2. BACKUP-BEFORE-MIGRATE** — gating `0 < version < SCHEMA_VERSION` (database.py:117) is correct (fresh=0 skips, current=7 skips). **WAL snapshot completeness verified empirically**: `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(FULL)` (database.py:152) synchronously flushes all WAL frames into the main file *before* `shutil.copy2` of the single main file — I reproduced this with a 24KB un-truncated WAL and even a second open connection, and the copied-main-file-only backup contained every row + the correct `user_version`, with no orphaned `-wal` sidecar. The single-instance lock (P0e) also rules out a real concurrent writer in production. Copying while `conn` is open is safe (FULL completed first). Best-effort WARN-not-block (database.py:155-157, catches `OSError`/`sqlite3.Error`) is the right call for a downloadable app. `from_version` is an `int` read from `PRAGMA user_version` (database.py:104) — not user-controlled, no injection/path concern in the f-string filename (database.py:150). `.bak-vN` is keyed by `from_version` so each upgrade-from-version writes exactly one file and re-running overwrites idempotently — bounded, no unbounded accumulation.

**3. backup_database(dest)** — checkpoint runs on the fresh `_connect` and the FULL checkpoint completes *synchronously inside* `conn.execute` (database.py:166) before the `with` closes, so the flush is durably in the main file by the time `shutil.copy2` (database.py:167) runs after close. `os.makedirs(parent, exist_ok=True)` guarded by `if parent` (database.py:163-164) handles a bare filename safely. `test_backup_database_export` confirms the export is a usable DB at `SCHEMA_VERSION`. Returns `dest`. Correct.

**4. INTERACTION** — `acquire_lock` (main.py:958) runs before `Database(db_path)` (main.py:973), so the guard/backup execute under the single-instance lock as intended — no concurrent-writer hazard during checkpoint/copy. Fresh-DB path (version 0): guard skipped (`0 > 7` false), backup skipped (`0 < 0` false) — `test_fresh_db_makes_no_backup` + `test_reopen_current_db_no_backup_no_error` confirm zero behavior change for the normal fresh/current case.

**5. EDGE** — Zero-byte file: reads `user_version = 0` → treated as fresh, no guard, no backup (verified). Corrupt non-SQLite file: raises `sqlite3.DatabaseError` from the first statement in `_init_db` — but this is *pre-existing* behavior unchanged by P2d (base-table creation already ran before this PR), and there's nothing to guard/back-up anyway. Read-only FS: WAL pragma already degrades via the `except sqlite3.OperationalError: pass` at database.py:55-56; backup failure degrades via the WARN path (database.py:155). Both degrade gracefully.

### Findings
No blocker/major/minor issues found.

- `[nit] backend/infrastructure/database.py:155` — `_backup_before_migrate` catches `(OSError, sqlite3.Error)`; an unexpected non-OSError/non-sqlite exception during checkpoint/copy (e.g. a `shutil` edge or a `MemoryError`) would propagate and brick `__init__`, defeating the "never block the upgrade" intent. Extremely unlikely in practice given copy2/checkpoint failure modes are OSError/sqlite3.Error. Fix (optional): broaden to `except Exception` for true best-effort, matching the repo's swallow-and-log convention used by the job writers. — Judgment call; the narrow catch is also defensible.

**Verdict:** Clean — the downgrade guard, WAL-complete backup (empirically verified), and export are correct and well-tested; ship it. (One optional nit on the backup exception breadth.)

"""Person-detection rally cue — torchvision detector + ByteTrack + motion windowing.

Extracted verbatim from `yolo_hybrid` (fusion P1, `docs/MULTI_SIGNAL_FUSION_PLAN.md`):
the **person cue is a swappable producer**, not logic baked into one segmenter. Today
`HybridYoloSegmenter` is the only consumer (it delegates Stage-1 here, byte-identically);
next the fusion segmenter consumes the SAME producer over shuttle-seeded windows, and
later the learned classifier reads its persisted features. Keeping detection here (not in
the segmenter) is what makes "add a cue = register a producer" true.

Behaviour is unchanged from the in-lined version — only the home moved:
  - `lazy_load_model`               — load a permissively-licensed torchvision detector
  - `make_tracker`                  — a fresh supervision ByteTrack per chunk
  - `detect_and_track`              — one frame → confident persons with persistent IDs
  - `extract_action_windows_from_chunk` — a chunk → high-motion candidate windows
    with the motion stats (`peak/mean_velocity`, `active_frame_count`, `track_id_count`)
    that feed the candidate `stats` JSON and the learned fusion features.

The model libs (torchvision, supervision) are imported lazily inside the methods, and the
detector weights load only on first detection (`lazy_load_model`) — so importing the
segmenter registry never loads a detector model, and tests can mock the seams. (torch + cv2
are top-level imports here; CI's import-smoke stubs them, and they're present on the GPU
machine.) Licensing: torchvision detectors are BSD + COCO weights; ByteTrack is MIT
(ADR-005; ultralytics' AGPL YOLO was dropped).
"""
import logging
from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

import cv2
import torch

from backend.utils.geometry import get_velocity_normalised

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

# torchvision detection models are trained on COCO where the "person" category is
# class 1 (index 0 is the implicit background). NB: ultralytics YOLO used class 0 —
# this off-by-one is the classic gotcha when swapping detectors. Keep it named.
_PERSON_CLASS_ID = 1

# Permissively-licensed torchvision detectors selectable via config
# `indexing.detector_model`. All are BSD-licensed (code + COCO weights).
_DETECTOR_FACTORIES = {
    "fasterrcnn_resnet50_fpn": "fasterrcnn_resnet50_fpn",
    "fasterrcnn_mobilenet_v3_large_fpn": "fasterrcnn_mobilenet_v3_large_fpn",
    "retinanet_resnet50_fpn": "retinanet_resnet50_fpn",

### 648. assistant (2026-06-21T19:17:47.789Z)

"""The LIGHT tier imports WITHOUT torch installed (PACKAGING_PLAN.md P2e).

`backend.main` and the detector registry must import even when torch/torchvision are absent — the
LIGHT tier (Gemini / motion / cpu-mock) ships no torch. The person-detection cue is HEAVY-only and
never instantiated under LIGHT, so its torch usage is deferred into its methods. This spawns a fresh
interpreter with torch/torchvision BLOCKED at the import machinery to prove it.
"""

import os
import subprocess
import sys

REPO_ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))

_SCRIPT = r"""
import sys, importlib.abc

class _BlockTorch(importlib.abc.MetaPathFinder):
    def find_spec(self, name, path, target=None):
        top = name.split('.')[0]
        if top in ('torch', 'torchvision'):
            raise ModuleNotFoundError(f"blocked for the no-torch test: {name}")
        return None

sys.meta_path.insert(0, _BlockTorch())

# Hard assert torch truly can't be imported in this interpreter.
try:
    import torch  # noqa: F401
    print("FAIL: torch was importable despite the block"); sys.exit(2)
except ModuleNotFoundError:
    pass

# The LIGHT-tier import surface must hold without torch:
import backend.main  # noqa: F401  (must not import torch at module load)
import backend.pipeline.detectors.person_detector  # noqa: F401  (torch deferred into methods)
from backend.pipeline.segmenters.base import segmenter_registry
assert "motion" in segmenter_registry.list_available()
print("IMPORT_OK_NO_TORCH")
"""


def test_light_tier_imports_without_torch():
    env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONPATH": REPO_ROOT + os.pathsep + os.environ.get("PYTHONPATH", "")}
    proc = subprocess.run(
        [sys.executable, "-c", _SCRIPT],
        cwd=REPO_ROOT, env=env, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=120,
    )
    assert proc.returncode == 0 and "IMPORT_OK_NO_TORCH" in proc.stdout, (
        "backend.main / person_detector must import with torch absent (LIGHT tier).\n"
        f"stdout:\n{proc.stdout[-2000:]}\nstderr:\n{proc.stderr[-2000:]}"
    )

   $100/₹10k cap; teardown is priority #1. Known blocker: native-WASB returned 0 rallies on a
   fresh build last time — reproducing the 22-rally result needs the engine session's exact tuned
   `worker infer detector_impl=native` config; budget time to nail it.

2. P2b-install — a `uv tool install` / generated cross-platform install script (a clean torch-free
   LIGHT install now, thanks to P2e) + ship a truly-local default config.json + an uninstall manifest.

3. P2c first-run wizard — the 3-step wizard (Gemini key / compute / videos) in the khelsutra web/
   SPA, rendered from GET /api/capabilities. CROSS-REPO (avidullu/khelsutra, gitignored dist) —
   coordinate like P1b, then refresh backend/ui/ via scripts/bundle_ui.sh.

Then P3-cloud (Cloud Run dispatch + micromamba Dockerfile = P3a) and P4 (ONNX export to dissolve the
dual-torch env + BYO training + in-app annotation = the WASB-complete north star).

One coordination note: a sibling branch `serving/exposure-safety-preflight` reworks
startup.py/main.py exposure checks — when it opens a PR, make sure it keeps my P0c
EXPOSED_HOST_ALLOWLIST_LOOPBACK check.
